I don’t know that I’m just anxious for no reason when things are actually okay.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling fatigued or noticed a loss of energy, even when you haven’t been doing anything physically or mentally taxing?\n\nPatient: I don’t know… not in a way I’ve really tracked. I get tired sometimes, but I can’t say if it’s more than normal.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or worried when there isn’t anything specific happening, like when things are actually going fine?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not really. If things are actually fine, I don’t usually notice myself worrying. At least, not that I can say clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel anxious or worried about different parts of your life — like work, your health, or other responsibilities — even when you know, logically, that things are under control or not as bad as you fear?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I worry about things sometimes, but I can’t tell if it’s like… across everything, or more just when something comes up. I don’t think I’ve noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot about different things in your life — like work, your health, or other responsibilities — even when you know, deep down, that things aren't that bad or are actually under control?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I worry about a lot of different things all the time… or at least I haven’t noticed it that way. It’s hard for me to say.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or worried about many different things in your life — like work, your health, or other responsibilities — even when you know those things are actually okay or not as bad as you fear?\n\nPatient: I don’t think I can say that happens often. I mean… I get worried sometimes, sure, but not really about many different things when they’re okay. I haven’t noticed it like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you worry a lot about different areas of your life, even small things, and that it's hard to control those worries even when you know they might not make sense?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure. I don’t think it’s like… constant worrying about everything. Sometimes I get stuck on things, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s a lot of different areas or that I can’t control it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Disorganized_Abnormal_Behavior\n  description: A state where behavior and motor control are severely disturbed, appearing as movements or reactions inappropriate to the situation.\n  manifestation: There are times I realize I’ve been pacing the same path from the kitchen to the front door, rubbing my thumb against my fingers and checking the peephole over and over, even though nothing has actually happened.\n- key: S023\n  name: Negative_Symptoms\n  description: A state where emotions, motivation, speech, or social interest that should normally be present are diminished or lost.\n  manifestation: My sister will tell me something funny or upsetting and I know I should react, but my face feels blank and it takes effort to answer with more than a few words.\n- key: S025\n  name: Loss_of_Interest\n  description: A state where interest or pleasure in previously enjoyed activities is markedly diminished.\n  manifestation: I used to plan brunches, paint in the spare room, go hiking, and sign up for networking events, but now those things feel like tasks I’m pretending to care about.\n- key: S026\n  name: Weight_Appetite_Change\n  description: Significant increase or decrease in appetite or weight.\n  manifestation: My appetite has been all over the place, but mostly I skip real meals during the day and then realize my clothes are looser and I’ve been living on coffee, toast, and whatever is easiest.\n- key: S027\n  name: Sleep_Disturbance\n  description: A state where there is a significant change in the quantity or quality of sleep.\n  manifestation: Most nights I lie awake for hours listening to the house settle, then wake up around three or four and can’t get back to sleep before work.\n- key: S028\n  name: Psychomotor_Changes\n  description: Observable increase or decrease in psychomotor activity.\n  manifestation: At work I move so slowly some mornings that opening spreadsheets, typing responses, or walking to a meeting feels like I’m pushing through wet cement.\n- key: S030\n  name: Worthlessness_Guilt\n  description: Feelings of worthlessness or excessive or inappropriate guilt (which may be delusional; not merely self-reproach or guilt about being sick).\n  manifestation: I came in because I can’t stop feeling like everything that goes wrong is my fault, like I’m a bad employee and a bad homeowner who deserves to lose what I worked for.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: psychotic_symptoms and depressive_episode occur concurrently\n  manifestation: During the same months I was barely eating, sleeping badly, and feeling like a failure, I was also hearing the woman’s voice and believing people were watching for mistakes with the house and my job.\n- key: additional_requirement_2\n  name: Additional Requirement 2\n  description: depressive_episode is present for the majority of the total duration of the active and residual portions of the illness (Criterion C)\n  manifestation: Looking back, the low mood and loss of interest have been there for almost the whole time since this started, with only brief periods where I could function a little better.\n- key: additional_requirement_3\n  name: Additional Requirement 3\n  description: delusions_and_hallucinations occur for 2 or more weeks in the absence of a major mood episode (Criterion B)\n mortgage company and my coworkers were monitoring me.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  description: required\n  manifestation: I’ve missed social plans, let the townhouse get messier than I can stand, and made enough mistakes at work that my supervisor started checking in on my performance.
